\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{113}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{121}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{122}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{123}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{131}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{132}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{133}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}.

We have that

\Sigma_{11}\preceq 3(\Sigma_{111}+\Sigma_{122}+\Sigma_{133}).

Thus, looking at the individual terms,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{111}

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{i}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{i}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}u(X_{i})\phi(Z_{i})\phi(Z_{i})^{*}u(X_{i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\preceq\bar{u}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

The other two diagonal terms require more care for
\Sigma_{122}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{122}

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\psi(X_{i})w(Z_{i})^{2}\psi(X_{i})^{*}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Big).

Recall that, for general
u,v

\left\langle u,\,S_{z}v\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}=\mathbb{E}_{Z}[u(Z)v(Z)].

Then,

\displaystyle\hphantom{=}\mathbb{E}\Big(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\psi(X_{i})w(Z_{i})^{2}\psi(X_{i})^{*}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left(w(Z_{i})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\psi(X_{i})\otimes\psi(X_{i})^{*}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}|Z_{i}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\left(\left\langle\phi(Z_{i}),\,w\right\rangle^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\right)T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}),\,S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}),\,(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle\preceq\left\langle(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}),\,(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle\preceq\bar{v}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}\kappa_{x}^{2}.

Thus,
\Sigma_{122}\preceq\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}
. Lastly, for
\Sigma_{133}
we can write

\displaystyle\Sigma_{133}

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}w\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}w\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})w(Z_{i})\}\Big)\otimes\Big(\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})w(Z_{i})\}\Big)^{*}

\displaystyle\preceq\bar{w}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

We can now combine the three diagonal terms, yielding

\displaystyle\Sigma_{11}

\displaystyle\preceq 3\bar{u}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+3\bar{w}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+3\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}

\displaystyle\preceq 3(\bar{u}^{2}+\bar{w}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+3\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}.

2.

For
\Sigma_{22}
, we use that
\varepsilon_{i}\leq\bar{\sigma}
. Then,

\displaystyle 0\preceq\Sigma_{22}

\displaystyle\preceq\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathbb{E}\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\}\otimes\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\}^{*}

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathbb{E}\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\otimes\phi(Z_{i})^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}

\displaystyle=\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

We collect results as

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle\preceq 2(\Sigma_{11}+\Sigma_{22})

\displaystyle\preceq 6(\bar{w}^{2}+\bar{u}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+6\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}+2\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(6\bar{w}^{2}+6\bar{u}^{2}+2\bar{\sigma}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+6\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}.\quad\qed

D.2
Covariance lower bound

We derive the variance lower bound of
Z
, where
Z
is a Gaussian element in
{\mathcal{H}_{x}}
with covariance
\Sigma
.

Assumption D.1

(Local width bound assumptions)
.

For our analysis we require that
\mu
and
\lambda
adhere to the following relationship

1.

\mu,\lambda<1
,

2.

\lambda^{(2\alpha+1)\wedge 2}\lesssim_{C_{\sigma,K}}\mu\lesssim_{C_{K}}\ \lambda
,

where
C_{\sigma,K}
and
C_{K}
are constants depending on
\sigma
and kernel constants, respectively.

Lemma D.2

(Covariance lower bound)
.

Suppose
D.1
holds.
Let
Z
be a Gaussian random element of
\mathcal{H}_{x}
with covariance
\Sigma
, and suppose
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{2}|Z_{i})\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}
almost surely. Then with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\lVert Z\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}-\left\{2+\sqrt{2\ln(1/\eta)}\right\}\sqrt{\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}},

where
\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu)=\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

Proof.

We lower bound
\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert
via the identity

\{\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert)\}^{2}=\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert^{2})-\mathbb{E}\{\lVert Z\rVert-\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert)\}^{2}

then appeal to
Lemma

B.10
. Let
B_{\mathcal{H}_{x}}
be the unit ball in
\mathcal{H}_{x}
.

1.

To upper bound
\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert-\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert)^{2}
, we express
\left\lVert Z\right\rVert
as the supremum of a Gaussian process:
\left\lVert Z\right\rVert=\sup_{t\in B_{\mathcal{H}_{x}}}\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle=\sup_{t\in B_{\mathcal{H}_{x}}}G_{t}
. By the variance upper bound

\sigma^{2}_{T}=\sup_{t\in B_{\mathcal{H}_{x}}}\mathbb{E}\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle^{2}=\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{op}\leq\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda},

where the bound follows from

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle\preceq(6\bar{w}^{2}+6\bar{u}^{2}+2\bar{\sigma}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+6\bar{v}^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}.

Using
D.1
we can again focus on the
\bar{w}
term. Further it still holds that
\bar{w}^{2}\leq\frac{\underline{\sigma}^{4}}{72\bar{\sigma}^{2}}
.
The latter implies that
12\bar{w}^{2}\leq 2\bar{\sigma}^{2}
since

\frac{\underline{\sigma}^{4}}{72\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\leq\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{6}\iff\frac{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}\leq 12
and

12\bar{w}^{2}\leq 2\bar{\sigma}^{2}\iff\bar{w}^{2}\leq\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{6}.

Using this,

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle\preceq 2(6\bar{w}^{2}+6\bar{u}^{2}+2\bar{\sigma}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle\preceq 2(12\bar{w}^{2}+2\bar{\sigma}^{2})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle\preceq 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\right\rVert_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{4\lambda}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}\|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{4\lambda}=\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}.

For
\mathbb{E}\|Z\|^{2}=\tr\Sigma
we can use the same derivations and find that

\displaystyle\tr\Sigma

\displaystyle\leq\tr 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}=8\bar{\sigma}^{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu),

where is defined as
\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu)=\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.
Thus,
\left\langle Z,\,t\right\rangle
is almost surely bounded on
B_{\mathcal{H}_{x}}
and by combining the two inequalities of Borell’s inequality with a union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}\geq u\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(|\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert|\geq\sqrt{u}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{u}{2\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}\right)=2\exp\left(-\frac{u}{\frac{4\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}\right).

By integrating the tail,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{(\left\lVert Z\right\rVert-\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert)^{2}\geq u\right\}du\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{u}{\frac{4\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}\right)du=8\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}.

2.

We lower bound
\mathbb{E}(\lVert Z\rVert^{2})
by the variance lower bound (under the previously introduced regularity conditions), meaning that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\tr\Sigma

\displaystyle\geq\tr\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu).

3.

Combining the bounds, we get that

\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)-8\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}-\sqrt{8\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}.

In particular, choosing

u=\sqrt{2\|\Sigma\|_{op}\ln(1/\eta)},

we conclude that with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\|Z\|\geq\mathbb{E}\|Z\|-\sqrt{2\|\Sigma\|_{op}\ln(1/\eta)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|Z\|

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}-\sqrt{8\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}-\sqrt{2\|\Sigma\|_{op}\ln(1/\eta)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}-\sqrt{8\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}-\sqrt{4\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}\ln(1/\eta)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\underline{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{\tilde{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}-\left\{2+\sqrt{2\ln(1/\eta)}\right\}\sqrt{\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda}}.\qquad\qed

D.3
Local width upper bound

Lemma D.3

(Local width bound)
.

Suppose
D.1
holds. We can bound the local width of
\Sigma
with the local with of the covariance operator
T
by

\sigma(\Sigma,m)\leq\frac{\sqrt{8}\bar{\sigma}}{\lambda}\sigma(T,m)
and
\sigma(\Sigma,0)\leq\bar{\sigma}\sqrt{8\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu)}
, where
\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu)=\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

Proof.

Recall that under
D.1
, we showed in
Lemma

D.2
that

\Sigma\preceq 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\quad

and

\sigma(\Sigma,0)^{2}=\tr\Sigma\leq\tr 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}=8\bar{\sigma}^{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\lambda,\mu).

Now, for general
m
, since
S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\preceq S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S=T
we have that

\Sigma\preceq 8\bar{\sigma}^{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}TT_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\preceq\frac{8\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}T

and subsequently

\sigma(\Sigma,m)^{2}\leq\frac{8\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\sigma(T,m)^{2}.\qed

Lemma D.4

(Rate condition)
.

If
n\geq\max\{n_{1},n_{2}\}:=N_{\delta}
then,
\delta\leq 1/2

where

\displaystyle n_{1}

\displaystyle=16\max\{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{-1}\mu^{-2},\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)^{-1}\lambda^{-2}\mu^{-1/2}\},

\displaystyle n_{2}

\displaystyle=144l(\eta)^{2}\kappa\max\{\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)\mu^{-1/2},\frac{\kappa}{\mu\lambda}(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1)^{2},\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\}.

The effective dimensions are
\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)=\tr(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}T)
and
\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)=\tr((S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z}).

Proof.

We study how the high probability bounds of
\delta
scale with
n
,
\mu
, and
\lambda
. We have

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}}_{A}+\underbrace{\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}}_{B}+\underbrace{\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)}_{C}

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}_{1}

\displaystyle=\ln(2/\eta)\frac{\kappa}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\delta_{z}

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{z}\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n}}\vee\frac{4\kappa_{z}}{n\mu}\right\}

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)

\displaystyle=2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu^{1/2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}\right).

For
n>16\{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{-1}\mu^{-2}\vee\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)^{-1}\lambda^{-2}\mu^{-1/2}\}
the square root parts of the maxima dominate.
Thus,

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}_{1}

\displaystyle=\ln(2/\eta)\frac{\kappa}{n^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\delta_{z}

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{z}\ln(2/\eta)\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n}}

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)

\displaystyle=2\ln(2/\eta)\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}.

For
A
,

\displaystyle\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\kappa^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1)\leq 1/6\iff n_{A}\geq\frac{6l(\eta)^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\lambda\mu}(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1).

For
B
,

\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}+\frac{\kappa 2\delta_{z}^{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}=\frac{l(\eta)\kappa(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1)}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\kappa\cdot\bigl(2\kappa_{z}l(\eta)\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}\bigr)^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\frac{1}{n}.

It suffices to make each summand at most
\tfrac{1}{12}
. Therefore,
B\leq\tfrac{1}{6}
holds if

n_{B1}=\frac{36l(\eta)^{2}\kappa^{2}\bigl(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1\bigr)^{2}}{\lambda\mu},\qquad\text{and}\qquad n_{B2}=\frac{48l(\eta)^{2}\kappa\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Lastly,
C\leq 1/6\iff n_{C}\geq 144l(\eta)^{2}\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)\mu^{-1/2}.

One can clearly see that
n_{B1}>n_{A}
. Thus,

\delta\leq 1/2
if, in addition to the
n_{1}
used above

n\geq 144l(\eta)^{2}\kappa\max\{\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)\mu^{-1/2},\frac{\kappa}{\mu\lambda}(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1)^{2},\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\}.\qed

Assumption D.2

(Rate condition)
.

Assume
n
satisfies
n\geq\max\{n_{1},n_{2},N_{\delta_{z}}\}
where

\displaystyle n_{1}

\displaystyle=16\max\{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{-1}\mu^{-2},\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)^{-1}\lambda^{-2}\mu^{-1/2}\},

\displaystyle n_{2}

\displaystyle=144l(\eta)^{2}\kappa\max\{\kappa_{x}^{1/2}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)\mu^{-1/2},\frac{\kappa}{\mu\lambda}(2\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}+1)^{2},\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\},

\displaystyle N_{\delta_{z}}

\displaystyle=16\kappa_{z}^{2}\ln\left(2/\eta\right)\left\{\ln\left(2/\eta\right)\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\vee\mu^{-1}\right\}.

D.4
Bounded summand

Lemma D.5

(Bounded
U_{i}
)
.

U_{i}
is
a
-bounded almost surely, meaning

\|U_{i}\|\leq a=\frac{2\kappa b_{\mu,\lambda}+4\kappa\lambda^{-1/2}v_{\mu,\lambda}+2\kappa_{z}\mu^{-1/2}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle\|U_{i}\|

\displaystyle=\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\|

\displaystyle+\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\|

\displaystyle+\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\|

\displaystyle+\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\varepsilon_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}b_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{2\kappa}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{1}{\mu}}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\kappa_{z}\sqrt{\frac{1}{\mu}}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\kappa b_{\mu,\lambda}+4\kappa\lambda^{-1/2}v_{\mu,\lambda}+2\kappa_{z}\mu^{-1/2}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

The first inequality follows from the triangle inequality in combination with Lemmas
E.2
and
B.3
.
∎

Lemma D.6

(Dominating rate in
a
under
\lambda=\mu^{\iota}
)
.

Suppose
v_{\mu,\lambda}\in\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{2})\wedge 1}\big)
and we choose the regime
\lambda=\mu^{\iota}
with

\iota\geq\begin{cases}\dfrac{1}{2\alpha+1},&\alpha\in[0,\tfrac{1}{2}]\\[8.0pt]
\dfrac{1}{2},&\alpha\in(\tfrac{1}{2},1].\end{cases}

Then,

\|U_{i}\|\leq a=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}\Big).

Proof.

Since
\mu\leq\lambda
, the upper bound is controlled by

B_{1}=\frac{v_{\mu,\lambda}}{\sqrt{\lambda}\mu}\qquad\text{and}\qquad B_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}.

Consider the projected bias rate

v_{\mu,\lambda}\in\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{2})\wedge 1}\big).

Let
p:=(\alpha+\tfrac{1}{2})\wedge 1\in[\tfrac{1}{2},1]
. Then
B_{1}=\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{p-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{-1}\big)
, so

\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\lambda^{p-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{-1}\sqrt{\mu\lambda}\Big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{p}\mu^{-1/2}\big).

With the schedule
\lambda=\mu^{\iota}
, this becomes

\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}}=\mathcal{O}\big(\mu^{\iota p-\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Hence
B_{1}=\mathcal{O}(B_{2})
provided
\iota p-\tfrac{1}{2}\geq 0
, i.e.

\iota\geq\frac{1}{2p}=\frac{1}{2\big((\alpha+\tfrac{1}{2})\wedge 1\big)}\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}\iota\geq\dfrac{1}{2\alpha+1},&\alpha\in[0,\tfrac{1}{2}]\\[8.0pt]
\iota\geq\dfrac{1}{2},&\alpha\in(\tfrac{1}{2},1].\end{cases}

Under these conditions,
B_{1}\leq\text{constant}\cdot B_{2}
, so the bound is dominated by
B_{2}
, yielding

\|U_{i}\|\leq a=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}\Big).\qed

Lemma D.7

(Dominating rate in
a
by the variance scale under
\mu=\lambda/C
)
.

If
\mu=\lambda/C
with
C>1
and
v_{\mu,\lambda}\in\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{(\alpha+\frac{1}{2})\wedge 1}\big)
for
\alpha\in[0,1]
, then

\|U_{i}\|\leq a=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}\Big).

Proof.

By the projected-bias decomposition, the upper bound is controlled by

B_{1}=\frac{v_{\mu,\lambda}}{\sqrt{\lambda}\mu}\qquad\text{and}\qquad B_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}.

With
\mu=\lambda/C
we have

B_{1}=\frac{Cv_{\mu,\lambda}}{\lambda^{3/2}},\qquad B_{2}=\frac{\sqrt{C}}{\lambda},\qquad\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}}=\sqrt{C}\frac{v_{\mu,\lambda}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}.

Let
p:=(\alpha+\tfrac{1}{2})\wedge 1
. By hypothesis,
v_{\mu,\lambda}=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{p})
, hence

\frac{B_{1}}{B_{2}}=\sqrt{C}\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda^{p-\frac{1}{2}}\big).
Since
\alpha\in[0,1]
implies
p\in[\tfrac{1}{2},1]
, we have
p-\tfrac{1}{2}\geq 0
.
Consequently,
\|U_{i}\|\leq a=\mathcal{O}\big(1/\sqrt{\mu\lambda}\big)
.
∎

Corollary D.1

(Constant bound on
a
)
.

Depending on the regime
\lambda=\mu^{\iota}
or
\mu=\lambda/C
, if the requirement on
\iota
is satisfy, we can bound
\|U_{i}\|\leq a
by

a\leq\frac{\widetilde{M}}{\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}.

Here
\widetilde{M}
is a constant depending on the regime. For
\lambda=\mu^{\iota}

\widetilde{M}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\kappa C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}\|T^{-\alpha}h_{0}\|+\frac{4\kappa C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}+\frac{2\kappa_{z}C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}+\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}\right)

and for
\lambda/C=\mu

\widetilde{M}:=\tfrac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2\kappa C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}\|T^{-\alpha}h_{0}\|+\frac{4\kappa C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}+\frac{2\kappa_{z}C_{\alpha}}{1-c_{\delta}}C^{1/2}+\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}\right).

Here,
1-c_{\delta}
gives a lower bound on the denominator introduced by the bias argument. It depends on the specific regime. For example, for
\mu=\lambda/C
,

c_{\delta}=\frac{r\sqrt{\mu}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}=\frac{r}{\sqrt{C}}.

Appendix E
Bahadur representation

We derive a Bahadur representation, as a step towards deriving
Q
. In particular, we show the difference between
n^{1/2}(\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda})
and
n^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}(U)
is small, i.e. the residual vanishes. Here,

\displaystyle U_{i}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)+(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S

\displaystyle\quad+S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\varepsilon_{i}.

E.1
Helpful orderings

Lemma E.1

(Covariance operator bound)
.

If
S_{z}
is invertible, then we have the decomposition

\begin{pmatrix}S_{x}&S^{*}\\
S&S_{z}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I&S^{*}\\
0&S_{z}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}S_{x}-S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S&0\\
S_{z}^{-1}S&I\end{pmatrix}.

Moreover,
S_{x}\succeq S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S\succeq 0
.

Proof.

The decomposition can be checked by matrix multiplication under our maintained assumptions. Next, the lower bound
S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S\succeq 0
holds as
S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S
is the symmetric square of
S^{*}S_{z}^{-1/2}
. For the upper bound, note that
S_{x}-S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S
is symmetric, so it suffices to check non-negativity of eigenvalues. Suppose, to the contrary, that it has an eigenvector
u_{\nu}
with negative eigenvalue
\nu<0
. A straightforward computation using the decomposition from above then shows

\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
-S_{z}^{-1}Su_{\nu}\end{pmatrix}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}S_{x}&S^{*}\\
S&S_{z}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
-S_{z}^{-1}Su_{\nu}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
0\end{pmatrix}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}\{S_{x}-S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S\}u_{\nu}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\nu u_{\nu}^{*}u_{\nu}<0.

This contradicts the hypothesized positive-definiteness.
∎

Lemma E.2

(Bound on the regularized cross-covariance)
.

The operator norm of the conditional expectation operator is bounded:

\left\|(S_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}S\right\|_{op}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{x}}{\mu}}.

Proof.

Note that
S_{z}\preceq S_{z}+I\mu\implies S_{z}^{-1}\succeq(S_{z}+I\mu)^{-1}
.
Thus,

S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\preceq S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}S\preceq S_{x}.

Where the last
\preceq
is followed by Lemma
E.1
. Now,

S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S=\{(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S\}^{*}\{(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S\}\preceq S_{x}\implies\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S\|\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{x}}.

This means that

\left\|(S_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}S\right\|\leq\left\|(S_{z}+\mu I)^{-1/2}\right\|\cdot\left\|(S_{z}+\mu I)^{-1/2}S\right\|\leq\mu^{-1/2}\sqrt{\kappa_{x}}.\qed

E.2
High probability events

Lemma E.3

(Hilbert Schmidt bounds for primitive events)
.

\|\hat{S}-S\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
and
\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
are bounded each with probability
1-\eta
for
n\geq 4
by

\|\hat{S}-S\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa}{n}\vee\frac{2\kappa}{n^{1/2}}\right)\leq 8\ln(2/\eta)\frac{\kappa}{n^{1/2}}:=\tilde{\gamma}_{1}

and

\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{2\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}}{n}+\frac{\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}}{n^{1/2}}\right)\leq 4\ln(2/\eta)\frac{\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}}{n^{1/2}}:=\tilde{\gamma}_{2}.

Proof.

For both bounds, we utilize Lemma
B.8
. For

\|\hat{S}-S\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
, note that
\mathbb{E}(S_{i}-S)=0
and by the boundedness of the kernel
\|S_{i}-S\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq 2\kappa
and
\mathbb{E}\|S_{i}-S\|^{2}_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq(2\kappa)^{2}.

Let
A=4\kappa
and
B=2\kappa
; the bound follows from concentration. For
\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
note that
\mathbb{E}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})=0
. Also,

\|\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}
and
\mathbb{E}(\|\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2})\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\bar{\sigma}^{2}.
Let
A=2\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}
and
B=\kappa_{z}\bar{\sigma}
; the bound follows from concentration.
∎

Lemma E.4

(High-probability bound for preconditioned
Z
-covariances)
.

The Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the projected estimation error of
S_{z}
is bounded with high probability:
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|_{HS}\leq\delta_{z}
with probability
1-\eta
,
where

\delta_{z}=2\kappa_{z}\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n}}\vee\frac{4\kappa_{z}}{n\mu}\right\}.

A sufficient condition for ensuring
\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
is

n\geq 16\kappa_{z}^{2}\ln\left(2/\eta\right)\left\{\ln\left(2/\eta\right)\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\vee\mu^{-1}\right\}:=N_{\delta_{z}}.

Proof.

The proof follows directly from matching symbols to Lemma F.2 of
34
. The rate condition is simply the result of solving for
n
, in
\delta_{z}\leq 1/2
.
∎

Lemma E.5

(High-probability bound on the projected feature–noise)
.

The projected empirical mean of the feature-noise is bounded with high probability:

\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{3}
with probability
1-\eta
, where

\tilde{\gamma}_{3}=2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{n\mu}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n}}\right\}.

Proof.

Note that
\mathbb{E}\{(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}\}=0
and also
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|\leq\frac{\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{\mu}\implies A=\frac{2\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{\mu}
. For the second moment, let
(\nu_{j}^{z},e_{j}^{z})
be the eigendecomposition. Then

\mathbb{E}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i}\|^{2}\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\langle\phi_{Z_{i}},e_{j}^{z}\rangle^{2}}{(\nu_{j}^{z}+\mu)^{2}}=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\nu_{j}^{z}}{(\nu_{j}^{z}+\mu)^{2}}=\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\implies\text{B}=\bar{\sigma}\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}.

Again, following Lemma
B.8
with probability
1-\eta

\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z_{i}}\varepsilon_{i})\|\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\bar{\sigma}\kappa_{z}}{n\mu}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n}}\right\}=\tilde{\gamma}_{3}.\qed

Lemma E.6

(Compounded operator bound)
.

With probability
1-2\eta
the projected compounded feature noise
T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)
is bounded:

\left\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\right\|\leq\gamma_{1}=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\lambda\mu}.

Proof.

Note that,

\left\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\mu}\|(\hat{S}-S)^{*}\|\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|.

Invoking Lemma
E.3
plus an union bound gives the result.
∎

Lemma E.7

(Inverse estimation error)
.

Suppose,
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|=\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, then,

\|(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}.

Proof.

By the resolvent identity,
\forall l\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}=(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{l}+\sum_{r=1}^{l-1}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}.

Now, let
l\to\infty
.
Since
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|=\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
we have
\|S^{*}[(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{l}\|\to 0.

Thus,

\underbrace{(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}}_{A}+\underbrace{\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}}_{B}.

By hypothesis,
\|A\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}}{\mu}
and
\|B\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}^{2}}{\mu(1-\delta_{z})}\leq\frac{2\delta_{z}^{2}}{\mu}
. Thus,
\|A+B\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}.

∎

Lemma E.8

(Projected inverse estimation error)
.

Suppose
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|=\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then,
\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\left\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\right\}\right\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Proof.

By the resolvent identity,
\forall l\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}=(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{l}+\sum_{r=1}^{l-1}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}.

Now, let
l\to\infty
.
Since
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|=\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
we have
\|S^{*}[(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{l}\|\to 0.

Thus,

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\left\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\right\}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|

\displaystyle+\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{z}}{2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}\frac{\delta_{z}^{2}}{1-\delta_{z}}\leq\frac{\delta_{z}}{2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}+\frac{2\delta_{z}^{2}}{2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}}=\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{2\sqrt{\mu\lambda}},

where the first inequality in the last line follows from Lemma
B.3
with
A^{*}=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{1/2}
and by the hypothesis.
∎

Lemma E.9

(Sandwich error bound)
.

The projected error
T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)
is bounded with high-probability: with probability
1-\eta
,

\left\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|\leq\delta_{1}=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}.

Proof.

Note that,

\left\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\mu}\|(\hat{S}-S)^{*}\|^{2}.

Invoking Lemma
E.3
gives the result.
∎

Lemma E.10

(High-probability bound for the operator deviation)
.

The scaled estimation error of the regularized covariance operator
T_{\mu}
is bound with high probability: with probability
1-3\eta
,

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\right\|_{op}\leq\delta:=\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}+\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta),

where
\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,n)=2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu^{1/2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}\right).

Proof.

We can write

\displaystyle T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\}}_{(I)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\}}_{(II)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S}_{(III)}\}.

We aim to bound the following terms:

\displaystyle\text{(I)}\quad\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\pm T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right.

\displaystyle\quad+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)+\left.T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|}_{\text{(I.1)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|}_{\text{(I.2)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|}_{\text{(I.3)}}.

We continue by again analyzing the individual terms

(I.1)

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}}{\lambda\mu}\|(\hat{S}-S)^{*}\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}
with probability
1-\eta
using the hypothesis and Lemma
E.3
.

(I.2)

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}
with probability
1-\eta
using Lemma
E.3
again.

(I.3)

This becomes more involved. Note that
\mathbb{E}\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}=0
. Also,

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|\leq\frac{2\kappa}{\lambda}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|\leq\frac{2\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\lambda\mu^{1/2}}

by Lemma
E.2
, hence
A:=\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\lambda\mu^{1/2}}
. The second moment is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|^{2}\}\

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\tr(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S)^{*}(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S)\}\

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\tr S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\{\tr S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{i}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathbb{E}\{\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}S_{z}^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}=\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu).

Thus,
B^{2}=\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)
and with probability
1-\eta
, we can bound (I.3) with

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu^{1/2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}\right):=\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta).

Now combining this with (I.1) and (I.2) with probability
1-2\eta

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}+\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta).

We turn to the remaining terms.

\text{(II)}\quad\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\left\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\right\}\hat{S}\right\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

This follows directly by applying Lemma
E.8
.

(III)

Note that
\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|\leq\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|\|(\hat{S}-S)\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

This holds on the event from Lemma
E.3
and using Lemma
B.3
with

A^{*}=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{1/2}
.
Combining the three bounds with a union for the distinct high probability events involved, we have with probability
1-3\eta

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\right\|_{op}\leq\delta=\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}+\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta).\qed

Lemma E.11

(Linearization)
.

Suppose
\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta<1
.
Then, for all
k\geq 1

(\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1})u=A_{1}u+A_{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u+A_{3}T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}u,\quad\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda},\quad\|A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta,\quad\|A_{3}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{1-\delta}.

Furthermore, when
\delta\leq 1/2
then,
\|(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})u\|\leq 2\delta\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}u\|.

Proof.

By the iterated resolvent identity Lemma
B.1
,

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}+\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{r}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{r}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+A_{3}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

Now, using
\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta<1
,

\displaystyle\|A_{1}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}\|_{HS}\leq\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\|A_{2}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})\|_{HS}\leq\delta

\displaystyle\|A_{3}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})\}^{r}\|_{HS}\leq\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}\delta^{r}\leq\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}\delta^{r}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{1-\delta}.

If in addition we assume
\delta\leq 1/2
, then

\displaystyle\|(\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1})u\|

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{1}\|_{HS}\|u\|+\|A_{2}\|_{HS}\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|+\|A_{3}\|_{HS}\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda}\|u\|+(\delta+\frac{\delta^{2}}{1-\delta})\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta^{k}}{\lambda}\|u\|+\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|\underset{k\to\infty,\delta\leq 1/2}{\leq}2\delta\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|.\qquad\qed

Lemma E.12

(Projected inverse estimation error with noise bound)
.

Suppose
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|_{HS}\leq\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then, with probability
1-\eta
,

\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\xi_{1}=\frac{\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Proof.

Using the resolvent identity, we can write for all
l\geq 1

\displaystyle(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{l}+\sum_{r=1}^{l-1}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}.

For
l\to\infty
using the same argument as in Lemma
E.11
,

\displaystyle\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|

\displaystyle=\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\{(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|\|\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\{(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}2\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}=\frac{\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.\qquad\qed

Lemma E.13

(Empirical projected noise bound)
.

Suppose
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|_{HS}\leq\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then, with probability at least
1-3\eta
it holds that

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{\phi(Z)\varepsilon\}\right\|\leq\gamma=\frac{\kappa 2\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Proof.

Note that,

\displaystyle T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{\phi_{Z}\varepsilon\}\pm T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{\phi_{Z}\varepsilon\}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)\pm T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)}_{\text{II}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)}_{\text{III}}.

(I)

In the first term,

\displaystyle\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{\lambda}\|\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}.

This follows by the event from Lemma
E.5
, using the assumption and the same application of the resolvent identity as in Lemma
E.12
.

(II)

In the second term,

\displaystyle\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\|(\hat{S}-S)^{*}\|\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}

on the events from Lemmas
E.3
and
E.5
.

(III)

In the third term,

\displaystyle\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}},

on the event from Lemma
E.3
, using Lemma
B.3
with
A^{*}=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{1/2}
.

Combining all three bounds, we get with probability at least
1-3\eta

\gamma=\frac{\kappa 2\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.\qed

E.3
Main result

Lemma E.14

(Abstract Bahadur representation)
.

Suppose

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{\phi(Z)\varepsilon\}\right\|\leq\gamma,\quad\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|\leq\delta_{z}\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\right\|_{op}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{2},

\left\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\right\|\leq\gamma_{1},\quad\|\hat{S}-S\|\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{1},

and

\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\xi_{1}.

Then,

\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda}=\mathbb{E}_{n}(U_{i})+u,\quad\|u\|\leq 2\delta\gamma+\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}+\left(2\delta^{2}+R\right)\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|

with

R:=\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\left\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}/2\right\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\big\}

and

\displaystyle U_{i}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)+(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S

\displaystyle+S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-S_{z,i})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\varepsilon_{i}.

Subsequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}(U)

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)+(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}).

Proof.

We proceed in steps

1.

First, we decompose

\hat{h}=\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{Y\phi(Z)\},\quad h_{\mu,\lambda}=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\{Y\phi(Z)\}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\{h_{0}(X)+\varepsilon\}\phi(Z)]-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)+\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=(i)+(ii).

Here,
(i)=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)
and
(ii)=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}.

2.

Observe that
(i)=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})P_{n}+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}P_{n}
where
P_{n}=\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon).
This follows by writing

\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}.

Invoking Lemma
E.11
and the assumed high probability events, the first term can be bounded by

\|(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})P_{n}\|\leq 2\delta\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}P_{n}\|\leq 2\delta\gamma.

Developing the remaining term,

\displaystyle T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}P_{n}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\pm T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon),

where by hypothesis
\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\gamma_{1}
and
\|T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}S^{*}\{(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)\|\leq\xi_{1}.

3.

Next, we show that
(ii)=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})Q_{n}+T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}Q_{n}
for
Q_{n}=(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})
. This argument is more involved. Note that

\displaystyle\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{0}+(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})T_{\mu}h_{0}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\hat{T}_{\mu}h_{0}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}T_{\mu}h_{0}

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{0}-\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{0},

where in the last line we use the resolvent identity to write

\displaystyle(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})T_{\mu}h_{0}=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}T_{\mu}h_{0}=\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(T_{\mu}-\hat{T}_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}=-\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}.

To conclude the argument, we note that

\displaystyle-\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{0}\pm T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}

\displaystyle=(T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})h_{\mu,\lambda}+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(-h_{\mu,\lambda})+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle=(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(-h_{\mu,\lambda})+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}Q_{n}.

By linearization and the high probability events,

\|(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})Q_{n}\|\leq 2\delta\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}Q_{n}\|\leq 2\delta^{2}\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|.

Developing the remaining term,

\displaystyle T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}Q_{n}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}).

We apply Lemma
B.2
to the expression giving us

\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S.

Now we utilize Lemma
B.2
with

\hat{A}=\hat{S}^{*},\quad A=S^{*},\qquad\hat{B}=(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1},\quad B=(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1},\qquad\hat{C}=\hat{S},\quad C=S,

and differences

\Delta A=\hat{S}^{*}-S^{*}:=\Delta S^{*},\quad\Delta B=(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1},\quad\Delta C=\hat{S}-S:=\Delta S.

Then, Lemma
B.2
allows us to write

\displaystyle\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}

\displaystyle=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S+S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta C+S^{*}\Delta BS+S^{*}\Delta B\Delta C

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta A(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S+\Delta A(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta C+\Delta A\Delta BS+\Delta A\Delta B\Delta C.

Thus, the difference is

\displaystyle\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S

\displaystyle=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta S+S^{*}\Delta BS+S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S+\Delta S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta S

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta S^{*}\Delta BS+\Delta S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S.

Let

\Delta B=\underbrace{(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}}_{\Delta_{1}B}+\underbrace{\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\{(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\}^{r}}_{\Delta_{2}B}.

Note the application of Lemma
E.7
. Thus,
\|\Delta B\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}.

Since
S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta S
,
\Delta S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S
, and
S^{*}\Delta_{1}BS
are part of the Bahadur representation, we control the following terms with the assumed events and Lemma
B.3
taking
A^{*}=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{1/2}
.

1.

T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\Delta_{2}BS
:

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\Delta_{2}BS\|\leq\kappa\frac{\delta_{z}^{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}(1-\delta_{z})}\leq\kappa\frac{\delta_{z}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

2.

T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S
:

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}.

3.

T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta S
:

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\Delta S\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}.

4.

T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}\Delta BS
:

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}\Delta BS\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})\kappa}{\lambda\mu}.

5.

T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S
:

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\Delta S^{*}\Delta B\Delta S\|\leq\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\lambda\mu}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}.

Together, the difference
\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S.
, without the terms accounted for in the Bahadur, can be bounded by the sum of the five bounds

\displaystyle\kappa\frac{\delta_{z}^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}+\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})\kappa}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\lambda\mu}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2}\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\}.

Together with the first part of the proof,

\|u\|\leq 2\delta\gamma+\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}+\left[2\delta^{2}+\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2}\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\}\right]\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|.

∎

Theorem E.1

(Bahadur representation)
.

Suppose
n\geq\max\{N_{\delta_{z}},N_{\delta}\}
, then, with probability
1-5\eta
,
\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda}=\mathbb{E}_{n}(U)+u
for some
u
with
\|u\|\lesssim V(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta)+B(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta,h_{0}):=\Delta_{U}
, where

\displaystyle l(\eta)

\displaystyle=\ln(2/\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\tr((S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z}),

\displaystyle V(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{3/2}l(\eta)^{3}}{n^{3/2}\lambda^{3/2}\mu^{1/2}}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}\ \vee\ \frac{\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)}{\sqrt{n\lambda\mu}}\right),

\displaystyle B(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta,h_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n\lambda\mu}\vee\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)^{2}\right)\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|,

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)

\displaystyle=2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu^{1/2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu),

\displaystyle=\tr T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}ST_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}=\tr\{(T_{\mu}+\lambda)^{-2}T_{\mu}\},\quad T_{\mu}=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S.

Proof.

Recall the following bounds from the high probability events in Lemma
E.14
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\gamma_{1}}\tilde{\gamma_{2}}}{\lambda\mu},

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa 2\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}},

\displaystyle\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}.

Note that for these bounds to hold, each of the primitive events needs to hold. Those are,
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
,
\tilde{\gamma}_{2}
,
\tilde{\gamma}_{3}
each with probability
1-\eta
. The
\delta_{z}
bound holds by Lemma
E.4
with probability
1-\eta
and
\delta
holds on the events
\tilde{\gamma}_{1}
,
\delta_{z}
and a Bernstein inequality with probability
1-\eta
again. Thus, using a union bound with probability
1-5\eta
, all the bounds hold and

\|u\|\leq 2\delta\gamma+\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}+\left[2\delta^{2}+\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\biggl\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2}\biggl\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\biggl\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\biggl\}\right]\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|.

Now, let
l(\eta)=\ln(2/\eta)
collecting terms while suppressing constants,

\displaystyle\delta\gamma+\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{3/2}l(\eta)^{3}}{n^{3/2}\lambda^{3/2}\mu^{1/2}}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}\ \vee\ \frac{\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)}{\sqrt{n\lambda\mu}}\ \vee\ \frac{l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}l(\eta)^{2}}{n\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{3/2}l(\eta)^{3}}{n^{3/2}\lambda^{3/2}\mu^{1/2}}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}\ \vee\ \frac{\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)}{\sqrt{n\lambda\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\delta\gamma+\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}\lesssim\left(\frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{3/2}l(\eta)^{3}}{n^{3/2}\lambda^{3/2}\mu^{1/2}}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda}\ \vee\ \frac{\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}l(\eta)^{2}}{n\lambda\mu}\ \vee\ \frac{\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)l(\eta)}{\sqrt{n\lambda\mu}}\right):=V(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta).

For the terms involving
\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|
we can write

\displaystyle\left[2\delta^{2}+\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2}\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\}\right]\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\{\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n\lambda\mu}\vee\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)^{2}\vee\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\vee\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu}\right\}\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\{\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n\lambda\mu}\vee\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)^{2}\right\}\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|\right)

\displaystyle\implies\left[2\delta^{2}+\frac{\delta_{z}}{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\{\delta_{z}(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2}\}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}\{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})(\kappa+\tilde{\gamma}_{1})\}\right]\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left\{\frac{l(\eta)^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{n\lambda\mu}\vee\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,\eta)^{2}\right\}\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|:=B(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta,h_{0}).

Combining this
\|u\|\lesssim V(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta)+B(n,\mu,\lambda,\eta,h_{0}):=\Delta_{U}
.
∎

Appendix F
Feasible bootstrap

We derive a feasible bootstrap in order to derive
R
.
Specifically, we aim to bound
\|Z_{\mathfrak{B}}-\mathfrak{B}\|
.
Recall that

\displaystyle Z_{\mathfrak{B}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{V_{i}-V_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij},\quad\mathfrak{B}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\hat{V}_{i}-\hat{V}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}\right)h_{ij}

with

\displaystyle V_{i}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})(Y_{i}-h_{\mu,\lambda}(X_{i}))+S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\}.

The feasible version contains

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\hat{\epsilon}_{i}+S_{i}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\hat{\varepsilon}\phi(Z)]

\displaystyle-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\hat{\varepsilon}\phi(Z)]\}.

This has already been derived in Appendix
A.2
.
To lighten notation, let

\displaystyle w_{i}

\displaystyle=S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})(Y_{i}-h_{\mu,\lambda}(X_{i}))+S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}),

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{i}

\displaystyle=\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\phi(Z_{i})\hat{\epsilon}_{i}+S_{i}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\hat{\varepsilon}\phi(Z)]-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\hat{\varepsilon}\phi(Z)],

\displaystyle w_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{i}-w_{j}}{\sqrt{2}},

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{w}_{i}-\hat{w}_{j}}{\sqrt{2}}.

F.1
Helpful orderings

Lemma F.1

(Regularized empirical block decomposition)
.

Let
\hat{S}_{x},\hat{S}_{z}\succeq 0
.
Then, for any
\mu>0
, we have the decomposition

\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}&\hat{S}^{*}\\[2.0pt]
\hat{S}&\hat{S}_{z}+\mu\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I&\hat{S}^{*}\\[2.0pt]
0&\hat{S}_{z}+\mu\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}&0\\[4.0pt]
(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}&I\end{pmatrix}.

Moreover, the following inequalities hold

\hat{S}_{x}\succeq\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\succeq 0.

Proof.

The left-hand side is

\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}&\hat{S}^{*}\\[2.0pt]
\hat{S}&\hat{S}_{z}+\mu\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}&\hat{S}^{*}\\[2.0pt]
\hat{S}&\hat{S}_{z}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\[2.0pt]
0&\mu\end{pmatrix}.

Thus, the left-hand side is positive semidefinite. The operator
\hat{S}_{x}-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}
is self-adjoint.
Assume, towards a contradiction, that it has an eigenvector
u_{\nu}\neq 0
with eigenvalue
\nu<0
.
Using the factorization above, a direct computation gives

\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
-(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}u_{\nu}\end{pmatrix}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}&\hat{S}^{*}\\
\hat{S}&\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
-(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}u_{\nu}\end{pmatrix}

=\begin{pmatrix}u_{\nu}\\
0\end{pmatrix}^{*}\begin{pmatrix}\{\hat{S}_{x}-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}\}u_{\nu}\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\nu\|u_{\nu}\|^{2}<0.

But
\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{x}&\hat{S}^{*}\\
\hat{S}&\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I\end{pmatrix}
is positive semidefinite, so its quadratic form cannot be negative. Thus,

\hat{S}_{x}-\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}\succeq 0.\qed

Lemma F.2

(Bound on the regularized empirical cross-covariance)
.

The preconditioned empirical cross-covariance is bounded in operator norm; specifically,

\big\|(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1/2}\hat{S}\big\|\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{x}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
F.1
, we have the operator inequality

\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}\preceq\hat{S}_{x}.

Taking operator norms of both sides, this yields

\big\|(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1/2}\hat{S}\big\|^{2}=\big\|\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu I)^{-1}\hat{S}\big\|\leq\|\hat{S}_{x}\|\leq\kappa_{x}.

Finally, taking square roots gives the claimed bound.
∎

Lemma F.3

(High-probability bound for the empirical operator deviation)
.

Assume
\delta\leq 1/2
. Then,

\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}\leq\delta_{B}=3\delta.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}

\displaystyle=\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\pm T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1})(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}+\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}+\delta\leq 2\delta^{2}+\delta\leq 3\delta.

\displaystyle\qquad\qed

Lemma F.4

(Empirical linearization)
.

We show an empirical version of
Lemma

E.11
. Suppose
\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{op}\leq\delta_{B}<1
. Then, for all
k\geq 1
,

({T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1})u=A_{1}u+A_{2}\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u+A_{3}\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}u,\quad\|A_{1}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta_{B}^{k}}{\lambda},\quad\|A_{2}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta_{B},\quad\|A_{3}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\frac{\delta_{B}^{2}}{1-\delta_{B}}.

Furthermore, when
\delta_{B}\leq 1/2
then
\|(\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}-T^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda})u\|\leq 2\delta_{B}\|\hat{T}^{-1}_{\mu,\lambda}u\|.

Proof.

By the iterated resolvent identity Lemma
B.1
,

\displaystyle T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}+\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{r}

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}+\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}+\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{r}

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}+A_{3}\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}.

Now, using
\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta_{B}<1
,

\displaystyle\|A_{1}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{k}\|_{HS}\leq\frac{\delta^{k}_{B}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\|A_{2}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\|_{HS}\leq\delta_{B}

\displaystyle\|A_{3}\|_{HS}

\displaystyle=\|\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}\{(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\}^{r}\|_{HS}\leq\sum_{r=2}^{k-1}\delta^{r}_{B}\leq\sum_{r=2}^{\infty}\delta^{r}_{B}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}_{B}}{1-\delta_{B}}.

If in addition we assume
\delta_{B}\leq 1/2
, then

\displaystyle\|(\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}-T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1})u\|

\displaystyle\leq\|A_{1}\|_{HS}\|u\|+\|A_{2}\|_{HS}\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|+\|A_{3}\|_{HS}\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{B}^{k}}{\lambda}\|u\|+(\delta_{B}+\frac{\delta_{B}^{2}}{1-\delta_{B}})\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{B}^{k}}{\lambda}\|u\|+\frac{\delta_{B}}{1-\delta_{B}}\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|\underset{k\to\infty,\delta_{B}\leq 1/2}{\leq}2\delta_{B}\|\hat{T}_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}u\|.\qquad\qed

F.2
High probability events

Lemma F.5

(Estimation error bound of the
Z
covariance operator)
.

\|\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
is bounded with probability
1-\eta
for
n\geq 4
by

\|\hat{S_{z}}-S_{z}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{z}=2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa_{z}}{n}+\frac{2\kappa_{z}}{n^{1/2}}\right)\leq 8\ln(2/\eta)\frac{\kappa_{z}}{n^{1/2}}.

Proof.

The proof follows from the same arguments as in Lemma
E.3
.
∎

Lemma F.6

(High-probability bound on the empirical feature–noise)
.

The empirical second moment of the feature–noise product is bounded: with probability
1-\eta

\mathbb{E}_{n}\|\phi(Z_{i})\epsilon_{i}\|^{2}\leq\gamma^{\prime},

where

\gamma^{\prime}\coloneqq 2\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}+2\ln\Big(\tfrac{2}{\eta}\Big)\left(\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{4}\kappa_{z}^{4}}{n}}\right).

Proof.

Let
\xi_{i}=\|\phi(Z_{i})\epsilon_{i}\|^{2}
. By boundedness,
0\leq\xi_{i}\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}
, and

\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}=\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\phi(Z_{i})\|^{2}\leq\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}.

Therefore,
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2}\leq\bar{\sigma}^{4}\kappa_{z}^{4}
. Applying Lemma
B.8
to
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
yields

\mathbb{E}_{n}\xi_{i}\leq 2\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}+2\ln\Big(\tfrac{2}{\eta}\Big)\left(\frac{2\bar{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{z}^{2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{4}\kappa_{z}^{4}}{n}}\right)

with probability at least
1-\eta
.
∎

Lemma F.7

(High-probability bounds for preconditioned
Z
-covariances)
.

With probability at least
1-\eta
, the deviation of the empirical covariance concentrates in the Hilbert–Schmidt norm:

\big\|(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\leq\delta_{z}^{\prime},

where

\delta_{z}^{\prime}:=2\ln\Big(\tfrac{2}{\eta}\Big)\left(\frac{4\kappa_{z}^{2}}{\sqrt{\mu}n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}\operatorname{tr}\big(S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big)}{n}}\right).

Moreover, with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S_{z,i}\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{2}\leq\delta_{\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}.

Proof.

Let,
\xi_{i}=(S_{z,i}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}
. First,
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}=0
. Next,
\|\xi_{i}\|_{HS}\leq\frac{2\kappa_{z}^{2}}{\sqrt{\mu}}
and

\mathbb{E}\|(S_{z,i}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}\|^{2}_{HS}\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\tr S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}.

Using
Lemma

B.8
, with probability
1-\eta

\|(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}\|_{HS}\leq 2\ln(2/\eta)\left(\frac{4\kappa_{z}^{2}}{\sqrt{\mu}n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}\tr S_{z}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}}{n}}\right):=\delta_{z}^{\prime}.

For the second statement, let
\xi_{i}=\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S_{z,i}\|^{2}
.
Clearly,
\xi_{i}\leq\frac{\kappa_{z}^{2}}{\mu}
. Furthermore,
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S_{z,i}\|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}^{2}\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathbb{E}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z,i}=\kappa_{z}^{2}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}.

Also,
E\xi_{i}^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{z}^{4}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}}{\mu}
. By

Lemma

B.8
, with probability
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S_{z,i}\|^{2}\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}+2\ln(2/\eta)\left\{\frac{2\kappa_{z}^{2}}{\mu n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{z}^{4}\tr(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{z}}{\mu n}}\right\}:=\delta_{\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}.\qquad\qed

Lemma F.8

(Empirical resolvent-weighted operator bounds)
.

Assume
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})\|=\delta_{z}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}
. Then, with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|S_{z,i}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big\|^{2}\leq 2\left\{\kappa_{z}^{2}\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}\right\}^{2}+4\ln\frac{2}{\eta}\left\{\frac{2\kappa_{z}^{4}}{\mu^{2}n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{z}^{6}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}n}}\right\}+2\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)=:\delta_{\mu}^{\prime},

where
\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu):=\mathrm{tr}\big((S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z}\big)
. In addition,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|S_{i}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big\|^{2}\leq 2\left\{\kappa\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}\right\}^{2}+4\ln\frac{2}{\eta}\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{\mu^{2}n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}n}}\right\}+2\kappa_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)=:\delta_{\mu}^{\prime\prime}.

Consequently, with probability at least
1-\eta
,

\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big\|^{2}\leq 4\ln\frac{2}{\eta}\left\{\frac{2\kappa^{2}}{\mu^{2}n}+\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)}{\mu^{2}n}}\right\}+2\kappa_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)=:\delta_{\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime}.

Proof.

For both parts we use the basic decomposition

\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|T(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big\|^{2}\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|T\big[(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big]\big\|^{2}+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\big\|T(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big\|^{2},

with the appropriate choice of
T
.

(i) Take
T=S_{z,i}
. By Lemma
E.7
and
\delta_{z}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}
,

\big\|S_{z,i}^{*}\big[(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big]\big\|\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}.

For the second term, let
\xi_{i}:=\|S_{z,i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|^{2}
. Then
\xi_{i}\leq\kappa_{z}^{4}/\mu^{2}
and

\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}=\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}\big(S_{z,i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z,i}\big)\leq\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathrm{tr}\big((S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z}\big)=\kappa_{z}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu),

which implies
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2}\leq(\kappa_{z}^{6}/\mu^{2})\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)
. Applying Lemma
B.8
yields the claimed bound.

(ii) Take
T=S_{i}^{*}
. By Lemma
E.7
and
\delta_{z}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}
,

\big\|S_{i}^{*}\big[(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}-(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\big]\big\|\leq\kappa\frac{\delta_{z}(1+2\delta_{z})}{\mu}.

For the second term, with
\xi_{i}:=\|S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|^{2}\leq\kappa^{2}/\mu^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}=\mathbb{E}\|S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}\big(S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{i}\big)=\mathbb{E}\big[\|\psi(X_{i})\|^{2}\mathrm{tr}\big((S_{z}+\mu)^{-2}S_{z,i}\big)\big]\leq\kappa_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu),

hence
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2}\leq(\kappa^{2}\kappa_{x}^{2}/\mu^{2})\mathfrak{n}_{z}(\mu)
. Lemma
B.8
gives the desired result for
\mathbb{E}_{n}\|S_{i}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|^{2}
, and the subsequent bound for
\mathbb{E}_{n}\|S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\|^{2}
follows by the same Bernstein step applied directly to
\xi_{i}
.
∎

F.3
Combining events

Lemma F.9

(Consistency)
.

Assume the same events hold as in the abstract Bahadur representation (Lemma
E.14
). In addition, assume that,

\displaystyle\|(\hat{S}_{z}-S_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{z}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\|(\hat{S}-S)(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{z}^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\|S-\hat{S}\|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{1},

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}_{n}[\phi(Z_{i})\epsilon_{i}]\|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{2}.

Denote the bias and projected bias as
\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|\leq b_{\mu,\lambda}
and
\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1/2}S(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})\|\leq v_{\mu,\lambda}
, respectively.
Then,

\|h_{\mu,\lambda}-\hat{h}\|\leq\Delta_{h_{\mu,\lambda}}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}b_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\delta_{z}^{\prime\prime}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\delta_{z}^{\prime}b_{\mu,\lambda}+\Delta_{U}.

Here,
\Delta_{U}
is the error of the Bahadur representation explicitly derived in Lemma
E.14
.
Also

\|h_{0}-\hat{h}\|\leq b_{\mu,\lambda}+\Delta_{h_{\mu,\lambda}}:=\Delta_{h}.

Proof.

By Lemma
E.14
,

\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda}=\mathbb{E}_{n}(U_{i})+\Delta_{U},

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}(U)

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)+(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda})

\displaystyle+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}(\phi_{Z}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}(h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}).

Using that
\|\hat{h}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|\leq\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(U_{i})\|+\Delta_{U}
, we can focus on the individual terms using the bias (
b_{\mu,\lambda}
), projected bias (
v_{\mu,\lambda}
), and Lemma
F.7
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}_{n}(U_{i})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}b_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\delta_{z}^{\prime\prime}v_{\mu,\lambda}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\tilde{\gamma}_{2}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}\delta_{z}^{\prime}b_{\mu,\lambda}.

Combining this with
Lemma

E.14
gives the result. For
\Delta_{h}=\|h_{0}-\hat{h}\|
, we can write
\Delta_{h}\leq\|h_{0}-h_{\mu,\lambda}\|+\|h_{\mu,\lambda}-\hat{h}\|\leq b_{\mu,\lambda}+\Delta_{h_{\mu,\lambda}}.

∎

Lemma F.10

(High-probability bound for the operator deviation)
.

With probability at least
1-4\eta
,

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})\right\|_{op}\leq\delta,

where

\delta=\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{z}\sqrt{\kappa_{x}}}{2\sqrt{\lambda}\mu}+\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{2\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}+\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,n),

and

\tilde{\delta}(\mu,\lambda,n)=2\ln\Big(\tfrac{2}{\eta}\Big)\left(\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\lambda\mu^{1/2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{n\mu^{1/2}}\mathfrak{m}(\lambda,\mu)}\right).

Proof.

We can write

\displaystyle T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{\mu}-T_{\mu})

\displaystyle=T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{\hat{S}^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\}}_{(I)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\}}_{(II)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}\{S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}-S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S}_{(III)}\}.

We aim to bound each of the three terms. We consider them individually.

\displaystyle\text{(I)}\quad\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\pm T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right.

\displaystyle\quad+T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)+\left.T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|}_{\text{(I.1)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|}_{\text{(I.2)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\right\|}_{\text{(I.3)}}.

We continue by again analyzing the individual terms

(I.1)

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(\hat{S}_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{z}-\hat{S}_{z})(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}\hat{S}\right\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}}{\lambda\mu}\|(\hat{S}-S)^{*}\|\leq\frac{\kappa\delta_{z}\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\lambda\mu}
with probability
1-\eta
using the hypothesis and Lemma
E.3
.

(I.2)

\left\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(\hat{S}-S)\right\|\leq\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}^{2}}{\lambda\mu}
with probability
1-\eta
using Lemma
E.3
again.

(I.3)

This becomes more involved. Note that
\mathbb{E}\{T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}=0
. Also,

\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|\leq\frac{2\kappa}{\lambda}\|(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|\leq\frac{2\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\lambda\mu^{1/2}}

by Lemma
E.2
, hence
A:=\frac{4\kappa\kappa_{x}^{1/2}}{\lambda\mu^{1/2}}
. The second moment is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\|T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\|^{2}\}\

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\tr(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S)^{*}(T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-1}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S)\}\

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\{\tr S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}(S_{i}-S)T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}(S_{i}-S)^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\{\tr S^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S_{i}T_{\mu,\lambda}^{-2}S_{i}^{*}(S_{z}+\mu)^{-1}S\}
